Hi, it's really nice to meet you too. Thank you so much for seeing me. 

Um, I guess I'm here because... well, I've been having a little bit of trouble at work. I'm a retail buyer, and... I really don't want to sound weird or anything, but I just can't seem to shake this feeling lately that people at my job, and maybe even some of our vendors, are... kind of tracking my buys? I'm sorry, does that make sense? I really just want to do a good job and not cause any problems for anyone, but it's been really hard to ignore. Is it okay that I came in for that?

Um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound crazy or anything, because I know how it sounds, but... yeah, sometimes I do.

Um... well, I wouldn't say "powerful," exactly. That sounds a little... arrogant, maybe? I really don't want to come across like I think I'm better than anyone else, because I know I have a lot to learn.

Um, well... I definitely haven't been hospitalized or anything like that! I promise. But, um... I guess things at work did get a little... complicated? 

I really hate to admit this, because I always try to be the best buyer I can be, but... during those times when I was feeling so energized, I kind of made a few missteps. I missed a couple of buying deadlines, which I know is bad, and I might have... well, I made some impulsive purchasing calls that upset a few of our vendors. My supervisor did give me a formal warning about it. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I'm usually so careful. 

And, um, I guess outside of work, I haven't really been myself either. I stopped going to my usual gallery nights and those weekend volleyball games with my friends. I just sort of pulled away. 

I'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful. I really am trying to get back on track and be a good employee again. Is it... is it normal to mess up like that when you're feeling that revved up? I just hope I haven't ruined everything.

Like, time I could use to make moves and get ahead on my buying orders. \n\nI just really want to impress my supervisor and do a good job for the company. That's not a bad thing, right? To have a lot of energy and want to be productive? I mean, if I feel fine during the day, is it okay that I'm not sleeping the full eight hours?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you only sleep a few hours, do you feel unusually powerful or especially gifted compared with other people?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say \"powerful,\" exactly. Um, a full week? Oh, no, definitely not that long. I mean, I don't think so.

Um, well... I guess I do take on a bit more, yeah. But I really try to make sure it's all helpful stuff for the store, you know? I don't want to seem like I'm just doing things randomly.

I'll hear this man's voice, and it sounds like he's right behind me. He'll say things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll just... laugh at the items I picked out. And I always turn around to apologize or explain myself, but... there's never anyone there. \n\nI know it's probably just stress from work, right? Like, my brain is just playing tricks on me because I want to do a good job so badly. I really try not to let it bother me, and I never talk back to it or anything. Is that... is that something that happens to people sometimes when they're stressed?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess... well, yeah, a little bit? But I always just thought of it as a positive thing, honestly. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nThere have been plenty of weeks recently where I sleep maybe... three or four hours a night? But I don't feel tired at all. I actually wake up feeling really wired and ready to go. I just start thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time, you know? Like, time I could use to make moves and get ahead on my buying orders. \n\nI just really want to impress my supervisor and do a good job for the company. That's not a bad thing, right? To have a lot of energy and want to be productive? I mean, if I feel fine during the day, is it okay that I'm not sleeping the full eight hours?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you only sleep a few hours, do you feel unusually powerful or especially gifted compared with other people?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say \"powerful,\" exactly. That sounds a little... arrogant, maybe? I really don't want to come across like I think I'm better than anyone else, because I know I have a lot to learn. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm feeling like that, I do get this sense that... like, I can just see things clearly? Like I can spot the next big trend before anyone else in L.A. even catches on. Sometimes I catch myself thinking that the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions to move. \n\nWhich I know is a little silly to say out loud, because I'm still just one of the junior buyers, and I have a great team above me. But in those moments, it just feels like I have this really special eye for it, you know? Like I'm really contributing something unique to the company. Is it normal to feel a little extra confident when you're working that hard? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too full of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that man's voice during times when you were not feeling especially energized or needing less sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes, actually. I hope it's okay to admit that. I know I said it was probably just stress, but... yeah, it doesn't only happen when I'm feeling super energized. \n\nThere was a stretch recently where things were a lot calmer. I was sleeping a normal amount, like seven or eight hours, and I didn't feel like I was, you know, controlling the whole market or anything. Just kind of a regular couple of weeks. But... I still heard the voice in the stockroom. And... I also kind of felt like people were tracking my buys during that time, too. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad, right? Like I should have just been feeling totally normal since I was sleeping. I really try to just focus on my work and not let it get in the way. I'm still doing a good job, I promise. I'd say the voice, and, you know, that feeling like people are tracking me, it's been going on for roughly ten months? \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound crazy or anything. No, I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I have days where I'm just tired from work, but not like... not depressed for two weeks straight or anything. I try to stay pretty positive, honestly. \n\nI really want to make sure I'm doing a good job, you know? So I try not to let myself get down. Is that... is that what you mean? Like, feeling really sad all the time? Because I don't think I've had that. I hope that's a good thing?\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior ever get you into serious trouble at work or lead to being hospitalized?\n\nPatient: Um, well... My supervisor did give me a formal warning about it. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I'm usually so careful. \n\nAnd, um, I guess outside of work, I haven't really been myself either. Like, time I could use to make moves and get ahead on my buying orders. \n\nI just really want to impress my supervisor and do a good job for the company. That's not a bad thing, right? To have a lot of energy and want to be productive? I mean, if I feel fine during the day, is it okay that I'm not sleeping the full eight hours?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you only sleep a few hours, do you feel unusually powerful or especially gifted compared with other people?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say \"powerful,\" exactly. That sounds a little... arrogant, maybe? I really don't want to come across like I think I'm better than anyone else, because I know I have a lot to learn. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm feeling like that, I do get this sense that... like, I can just see things clearly? Like I can spot the next big trend before anyone else in L.A. even catches on. Sometimes I catch myself thinking that the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions to move. \n\nWhich I know is a little silly to say out loud, because I'm still just one of the junior buyers, and I have a great team above me. But in those moments, it just feels like I have this really special eye for it, you know? Like I'm really contributing something unique to the company. Is it normal to feel a little extra confident when you're working that hard? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too full of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that man's voice during times when you were not feeling especially energized or needing less sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes, actually. I hope it's okay to admit that. I know I said it was probably just stress, but... yeah, it doesn't only happen when I'm feeling super energized. \n\nThere was a stretch recently where things were a lot calmer. I was sleeping a normal amount, like seven or eight hours, and I didn't feel like I was, you know, controlling the whole market or anything. Just kind of a regular couple of weeks. But... I still heard the voice in the stockroom. And... I also kind of felt like people were tracking my buys during that time, too. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad, right? Like I should have just been feeling totally normal since I was sleeping. I really try to just focus on my work and not let it get in the way. I'm still doing a good job, I promise. Is it... is it bad that it happens even when I'm feeling calm?\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been hearing that man's voice?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a little while now. I hope it's not too concerning, but... I'd say the voice, and, you know, that feeling like people are tracking me, it's been going on for roughly ten months? \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound crazy or anything. I mean, I've still been going to work every day and doing my best for the team the whole time. It's just something that's kind of been in the background, you know? Is ten months a really bad sign, or is that something we can fix? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I can get back to being my best self at work.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were tracking you, but you were not feeling unusually energized, unusually confident, or sleeping less than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I think I mentioned this just a second ago, but I want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nSo, yes, there was definitely a stretch... I'd say it was a few weeks, actually. I was sleeping normally, getting my full eight hours, and I wasn't feeling all revved up or super confident about the market. I was just feeling, you know, completely average. But during those few weeks, I was still hearing that voice in the stockroom, and I still had that... that feeling that people were tracking my buys. \n\nI know it's probably not what you want to hear, and I really wish I could say it went away when I was sleeping better. I'm sorry if that complicates things. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um, depressed? No, I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I have days where I'm just tired from work, but not like... not depressed for two weeks straight or anything. I try to stay pretty positive, honestly. \n\nI really want to make sure I'm doing a good job, you know? So I try not to let myself get down. Is that... is that what you mean? Like, feeling really sad all the time? Because I don't think I've had that. I hope that's a good thing?\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior ever get you into serious trouble at work or lead to being hospitalized?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I definitely haven't been hospitalized or anything like that! I promise. But, um... I guess things at work did get a little... complicated? \n\nI really hate to admit this, because I always try to be the best buyer I can be, but... during those times when I was feeling so energized, I kind of made a few missteps. I missed a couple of buying deadlines, which I know is bad, and I might have... well, I made some impulsive purchasing calls that upset a few of our vendors. My supervisor did give me a formal warning about it. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I'm usually so careful. \n\nAnd, um, I guess outside of work, I haven't really been myself either. I stopped going to my usual gallery nights and those weekend volleyball games with my friends. I just sort of pulled away. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful. I really am trying to get back on track and be a good employee again. Is it... is it normal to mess up like that when you're feeling that revved up? I just hope I haven't ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you find yourself talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I don't want to make it sound like I'm dominating the room or anything, because I really do value my coworkers' input. I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too full of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that man's voice during times when you were not feeling especially energized or needing less sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes, actually. I'll hear this man's voice, and it sounds like he's right behind me. He'll say things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll just... laugh at the items I picked out. And I always turn around to apologize or explain myself, but... there's never anyone there. \n\nI know it's probably just stress from work, right? Like, my brain is just playing tricks on me because I want to do a good job so badly. I really try not to let it bother me, and I never talk back to it or anything. Is that... is that something that happens to people sometimes when they're stressed?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess... well, yeah, a little bit? But I always just thought of it as a positive thing, honestly. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nThere have been plenty of weeks recently where I sleep maybe... three or four hours a night? But I don't feel tired at all. I actually wake up feeling really wired and ready to go. I just start thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time, you know? Like, time I could use to make moves and get ahead on my buying orders. \n\nI just really want to impress my supervisor and do a good job for the company. That's not a bad thing, right? To have a lot of energy and want to be productive? I mean, if I feel fine during the day, is it okay that I'm not sleeping the full eight hours?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you only sleep a few hours, do you feel unusually powerful or especially gifted compared with other people?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say \"powerful,\" exactly. That sounds a little... arrogant, maybe? I really don't want to come across like I think I'm better than anyone else, because I know I have a lot to learn. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm feeling like that, I do get this sense that... like, I can just see things clearly? Like I can spot the next big trend before anyone else in L.A. even catches on. Sometimes I catch myself thinking that the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions to move. \n\nWhich I know is a little silly to say out loud, because I'm still just one of the junior buyers, and I have a great team above me. But in those moments, it just feels like I have this really special eye for it, you know? Like I'm really contributing something unique to the company. Is it normal to feel a little extra confident when you're working that hard? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too full of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that man's voice during times when you were not feeling especially energized or needing less sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes, actually. I hope it's okay to admit that. I know I said it was probably just stress, but... yeah, it doesn't only happen when I'm feeling super energized. \n\nThere was a stretch recently where things were a lot calmer. I was sleeping a normal amount, like seven or eight hours, and I didn't feel like I was, you know, controlling the whole market or anything. Just kind of a regular couple of weeks. But... I still heard the voice in the stockroom. And... I also kind of felt like people were tracking my buys during that time, too. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad, right? Like I should have just been feeling totally normal since I was sleeping. I really try to just focus on my work and not let it get in the way. I'm still doing a good job, I promise. Is it... is it bad that it happens even when I'm feeling calm?\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been hearing that man's voice?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a little while now. I hope it's not too concerning, but... I'd say the voice, and, you know, that feeling like people are tracking me, it's been going on for roughly ten months? \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound crazy or anything. I mean, I've still been going to work every day and doing my best for the team the whole time. It's just something that's kind of been in the background, you know? Is ten months a really bad sign, or is that something we can fix? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I can get back to being my best self at work.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were tracking you, but you were not feeling unusually energized, unusually confident, or sleeping less than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I think I mentioned this just a second ago, but I want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nSo, yes, there was definitely a stretch... I'd say it was a few weeks, actually. I was sleeping normally, getting my full eight hours, and I wasn't feeling all revved up or super confident about the market. I was just feeling, you know, completely average. But during those few weeks, I was still hearing that voice in the stockroom, and I still had that... that feeling that people were tracking my buys. \n\nI know it's probably not what you want to hear, and I really wish I could say it went away when I was sleeping better. I'm sorry if that complicates things. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um, depressed? No, I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I have days where I'm just tired from work, but not like... not depressed for two weeks straight or anything. I try to stay pretty positive, honestly. \n\nI really want to make sure I'm doing a good job, you know? So I try not to let myself get down. Is that... is that what you mean? Like, feeling really sad all the time? Because I don't think I've had that. I hope that's a good thing?\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior ever get you into serious trouble at work or lead to being hospitalized?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I definitely haven't been hospitalized or anything like that! I promise. But, um... I guess things at work did get a little... complicated? \n\nI really hate to admit this, because I always try to be the best buyer I can be, but... during those times when I was feeling so energized, I kind of made a few missteps. I missed a couple of buying deadlines, which I know is bad, and I might have... well, I made some impulsive purchasing calls that upset a few of our vendors. My supervisor did give me a formal warning about it. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I'm usually so careful. \n\nAnd, um, I guess outside of work, I haven't really been myself either. I stopped going to my usual gallery nights and those weekend volleyball games with my friends. I just sort of pulled away. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful. I really am trying to get back on track and be a good employee again. Is it... is it normal to mess up like that when you're feeling that revved up? I just hope I haven't ruined everything.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you find yourself talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I don't want to make it sound like I'm dominating the room or anything, because I really do value my coworkers' input. But, um... people have mentioned that I can be a little... enthusiastic in meetings. \n\nLike, when I'm explaining a new product drop or a deal I've found, I just get so excited about it. And sometimes the words just come out really, really fast. I guess they've said I sort of talk over them sometimes, just because once I get going, I kind of don't notice if someone else is trying to jump in. \n\nI feel really bad about it, honestly. I don't mean to be rude or anything. I just get so passionate about the buys. I'm really trying to work on being a better listener, though! Do you think that's... I mean, is it okay to get that excited about work sometimes? I hope it doesn't make me sound like a bad team player.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy times, did your thoughts ever race so fast that it was hard to keep up with them?\n\nPatient: Um, racing thoughts? I don't... I wouldn't really call it that. I mean, I definitely have a lot of ideas when I'm feeling that good, especially about work and finding the best deals for the store. \n\nI guess sometimes my mind is just... really active? Like I'm just brainstorming really well. It's not like I can't keep up with my own head or anything scary like that. It's more just like I'm being super productive and creative all at once. \n\nI try to make sure I write everything down so I don't lose any good ideas. That's a good way to handle it, right? I just want to make sure I'm channeling all that energy into being a good buyer. I don't think having a lot of ideas is a bad thing, is it?\n\nDoctor: Have those unusually energized periods ever lasted a full week or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, a full week? Oh, no, definitely not that long. I mean, I don't think so. \n\nUsually, it's just, you know, a few days where I'm feeling really on top of things and getting a lot done. I don't think it's ever stretched out for a whole week or anything like that. I try really hard to pace myself so I don't get too carried away. \n\nI guess maybe four or five days at most? But then I usually settle back down and get back to my normal routine. That's pretty typical, right? Just having a few really good days in a row? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you find yourself taking on a lot more activities or projects than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I do take on a bit more, yeah. But I really try to make sure it's all helpful stuff for the store, you know? I don't want to seem like I'm just doing things randomly. \n\nIt's just that when I'm feeling that keyed up, I can't really just sit and handle one single task. I feel like I need to be doing more to be useful. So I'll be, like, emailing three different vendors at the same time, and drawing up a new buy plan, and then I'll start reorganizing all the product samples. And I do end up pacing around the office a bit while I'm doing it. It just feels like if I stop moving, I'll lose the thread of what I'm working on. \n\nBut it's all work-related! I'm just trying to be really thorough. Is that... is it bad to multitask that much? I really hope my boss just sees it as me being dedicated.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, do you take on many more projects or activities than you usually would?\n\nPatient: Um, I think we just talked about that a little bit, right? I hope I answered it okay before. But, um, yeah, I guess I do take on a lot more than usual. \n\nLike I was saying, when I'm feeling that keyed up, I just can't sit still and focus on one thing. It feels like I have to keep moving to be productive. So I'll be emailing three different vendors at once, trying to put together a new buy plan, and then I'll start reorganizing all the samples we have in the back. And I do end up pacing around the office a lot. It's just... if I stop moving, I feel like I'll lose the thread of everything I'm doing. \n\nBut I'm just trying to be a really good employee, you know? I want my boss to see how hard I work. It's okay to want to get a lot done, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you get much more done than usual, or mostly start lots of things without finishing them?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... I mean, I hope I'm finishing things. I really try to. But, like I mentioned, when I get that keyed up, I just can't handle doing just one task at a time. It feels like I'm wasting time if I'm only doing one thing, you know?\n\nSo, yeah, I'll be emailing those three vendors, and then working on the buy plan, and reorganizing the samples all at once. And pacing around. It's just... I feel like if I stop moving or focus on just one thing, I'll lose the thread of everything else I'm trying to juggle. \n\nI do get a lot started, for sure. I might bounce around between them a bit, but I really am just trying to be as productive as possible for the store. It's better to be doing too much than not enough, right? I hope my boss sees it that way, anyway. I'm doing okay explaining this, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did other people notice that you were much more active than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think they probably did. I mean, I know I keep bringing up the same stuff, sorry, but like I was saying with the emailing all those vendors at once and reorganizing the back room... it's kind of hard for people not to notice all of that, I guess. \n\nEspecially the pacing. A couple of my coworkers actually asked if I was okay because I was walking back and forth across the office so much while trying to keep the thread of that buy plan in my head. I just... I tried to play it off, you know? I told them I was just really in the zone. \n\nI really hope they just saw it as me being super dedicated to the store and not, like, being weird or disruptive. It's a good thing to be that engaged with your work, right? I'm really just trying to be a good team player. Did that answer what you were asking? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, yeah, I guess I did take on a lot more. I mean, I know I'm kind of beating a dead horse here, and I'm really sorry to keep bringing up the same examples! I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture.

Like they're purposely messing with the launch numbers just to make me look stupid. \n\nI know it probably sounds silly when I say it out loud, and my supervisor even sat down and showed me the reports to prove that's not happening, but... I just still feel it. I'm sorry, does that make sense? I really just want to do a good job and not cause any problems for anyone, but it's been really hard to ignore. Is it okay that I came in for that?\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that other people don’t seem to hear?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound crazy or anything, because I know how it sounds, but... yeah, sometimes I do. \n\nIt's usually just when I'm alone, like if I'm in the stockroom by myself, or driving back from a vendor meeting. I'll hear this man's voice, and it sounds like he's right behind me. He'll say things like, \"Move faster, Scott,\" or he'll just... laugh at the items I picked out. And I always turn around to apologize or explain myself, but... there's never anyone there. \n\nI know it's probably just stress from work, right? Like, my brain is just playing tricks on me because I want to do a good job so badly. I really try not to let it bother me, and I never talk back to it or anything. Is that... is that something that happens to people sometimes when they're stressed?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times recently when you felt unusually energetic and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess... well, yeah, a little bit? But I always just thought of it as a positive thing, honestly. I hope it's okay to say that. \n\nThere have been plenty of weeks recently where I sleep maybe... three or four hours a night? But I don't feel tired at all. I actually wake up feeling really wired and ready to go. I just start thinking that lying in bed is just wasting time, you know? Like, time I could use to make moves and get ahead on my buying orders. \n\nI just really want to impress my supervisor and do a good job for the company. That's not a bad thing, right? To have a lot of energy and want to be productive? I mean, if I feel fine during the day, is it okay that I'm not sleeping the full eight hours?\n\nDoctor: During those times when you only sleep a few hours, do you feel unusually powerful or especially gifted compared with other people?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say \"powerful,\" exactly. That sounds a little... arrogant, maybe? I really don't want to come across like I think I'm better than anyone else, because I know I have a lot to learn. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm feeling like that, I do get this sense that... like, I can just see things clearly? Like I can spot the next big trend before anyone else in L.A. even catches on. Sometimes I catch myself thinking that the whole sneaker market is basically just waiting on my decisions to move. \n\nWhich I know is a little silly to say out loud, because I'm still just one of the junior buyers, and I have a great team above me. But in those moments, it just feels like I have this really special eye for it, you know? Like I'm really contributing something unique to the company. Is it normal to feel a little extra confident when you're working that hard? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too full of myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that man's voice during times when you were not feeling especially energized or needing less sleep?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yes, actually. I hope it's okay to admit that. I know I said it was probably just stress, but... yeah, it doesn't only happen when I'm feeling super energized. \n\nThere was a stretch recently where things were a lot calmer. I was sleeping a normal amount, like seven or eight hours, and I didn't feel like I was, you know, controlling the whole market or anything. Just kind of a regular couple of weeks. But... I still heard the voice in the stockroom. And... I also kind of felt like people were tracking my buys during that time, too. \n\nI know that probably sounds bad, right? Like I should have just been feeling totally normal since I was sleeping. I really try to just focus on my work and not let it get in the way. I'm still doing a good job, I promise. Is it... is it bad that it happens even when I'm feeling calm?\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been hearing that man's voice?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a little while now. I hope it's not too concerning, but... I'd say the voice, and, you know, that feeling like people are tracking me, it's been going on for roughly ten months? \n\nI really hope that doesn't make me sound crazy or anything. I mean, I've still been going to work every day and doing my best for the team the whole time. It's just something that's kind of been in the background, you know? Is ten months a really bad sign, or is that something we can fix? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I can get back to being my best self at work.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voice or felt people were tracking you, but you were not feeling unusually energized, unusually confident, or sleeping less than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I think I mentioned this just a second ago, but I want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nSo, yes, there was definitely a stretch... I'd say it was a few weeks, actually. I was sleeping normally, getting my full eight hours, and I wasn't feeling all revved up or super confident about the market. I was just feeling, you know, completely average. But during those few weeks, I was still hearing that voice in the stockroom, and I still had that... that feeling that people were tracking my buys. \n\nI know it's probably not what you want to hear, and I really wish I could say it went away when I was sleeping better. I'm sorry if that complicates things. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um, depressed? I hope that's a good thing?\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your behavior ever get you into serious trouble at work or lead to being hospitalized?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I definitely haven't been hospitalized or anything like that! I promise. But, um... My supervisor did give me a formal warning about it. I feel terrible about it, I really do. I'm usually so careful. \n\nAnd, um, I guess outside of work, I haven't really been myself either.
